The tent-cloths of haircloth signify the coarse minds of men, which by the hidden judgment of God are preferred, however harshly, because they fear not to serve temporal cares, that they may [0271C] bear the winds and rains of temptations.

*Thou shalt make also tent-cloths,* etc. [n: (ID., ibid.)] Haircloth belongs to penitents, etc., *as far as* yet how great is the perfection of their hearts is declared when it is said: *The length of one tent-cloth,* etc.

[n: (ID., ibid.)] The tent-cloths of haircloth, or of goats' hair, etc., *as far as* nor do they receive the apostolic grace.

[n: (AUG., quaest. 108 in Exod.)] There are eleven hairy veils, etc., *as far as and whose sins are covered.*

*For a covering,* namely lest the inner things, tender and bright, be troubled by any adversity whatever; for otherwise neither does the fine linen shine, nor the scarlet glitter, nor is the hyacinth adorned with its blue colour: those therefore who advance in the bosom of the Church [0271D] ought not to despise their superiors, if they see them taken up with outward things.

VERS. 8.-- *The length of one tent-cloth. [n: (BEDA, lib. II de Tabernac., cap. 3, tom. II.)]* The length has the number ten, etc., *as far as* who nevertheless, according to the measure of human perfection, are exalted among men.

*The measure shall be equal. [n: (ID., ibid.)]* Because there is one faith in which the whole Church is saved, etc., *as far as* where the solid firmness of a wall was wanting.

[n: (ID., ibid.)] Consider also the measure of the curtains, etc., *as far as* So much for the curtains.

[n: (ID., ibid.)] The tent-cloth has thirty cubits of length, etc., *as far as* in that they are leaders of the flocks that follow their lords.

[0272A]VERS. 9.-- *The sixth tent-cloth in the front.* Mystically, however, in the front of the roof the sixth tent-cloth is doubled, when all who enter the holy Church are so initiated by faith and by the sacrament of the Lord's passion that they understand that they must always live by the imitation of it as well. For as it were at the entrance the sixth tent-cloth is doubled, that we may both obtain the sacraments of the Lord's passion and be instructed by his examples. Of the sacraments Peter says: *Who hath regenerated us unto a lively hope, by the resurrection of Jesus Christ from the dead.* And he says also of the imitation of the passion: *Christ therefore having suffered in the flesh, be you also armed with the same thought.*

VERS. 10.-- *Thou shalt make also fifty loops in the edge of one tent-cloth. [n: (BEDA, ibid.)]* As we have expounded these things above concerning the curtains, etc., *as far as* so much the more does he seek the help of his neighbour, [0272B] whereby he may be strengthened.

VERS. 11.-- *Of brass, wherewith the loops may be joined, that of all there may be made one covering,* etc. [n: (ID.)] Now the buckles are rightly of brass, made, that is, of a sounding metal, because the humble conscience of the just has a great voice with God. Whence the poor man of David, when he was in anguish and was pouring out his prayer not into the ears of men but in the sight of God: *O Lord,* (he says) *hear my prayer, and let my cry come to thee.*

VERS. 12.-- *But that which shall remain over,* etc. [n: (ID., ibid.)] That these things may be more fully understood, etc., *as far as* ten again of descent along the boards of the eastern wall.

VERS. 13.-- *And a cubit.* Because the tent-cloths passed beyond the curtains by one cubit [0272C] on the southern side of the house, and by another on the northern, and therefore reached to the ground, since these had thirty cubits of length and those two less.

VERS. 14.-- *Thou shalt make also a covering.* [n: (STRAB.)] The first covering was that of the curtains of fine linen, purple and scarlet; the second that of the eleven tent-cloths; the third of the reddened skins of rams; the last was of hyacinth-coloured skins. Now the curtains so covered that they came down at the sides, yet did not reach the ground; but the tent-cloths covered both roof and sides, and did reach the ground. But the reddened or violet skins covered the roof only, nor did they slope down to the other parts.

*Of skins,* etc. [n: (BED., ibid., cap. 4.)] The rams are [0272D] the doctors, the leaders of the Lord's flock. Whence: *Bring to the Lord, O ye children of God, bring,* etc. As if to say: O angels, appointed to this office, bring to the Lord into the heavens the imitators of the apostles. Hence also the sixth station of the people that went out of Egypt is named helin, that is, rams, in which there were twelve fountains and seventy palm trees, that so by name and by kind it might hold the figure of the apostles.

*Reddened. [n: (ID., ibid.)]* The skins of rams are reddened for covering the roof of the tabernacle, etc., *as far as* lest they fail amid oppressions, they fortify

*Of violet skins. [n: (ID., ibid.)]* The hyacinth is of a heavenly colour, etc., *as far as* that is, imitating the life of heaven.

VERS. 15.-- *Of setim wood. [n: (ID., ibid.)]* These boards [0273A] signify the apostles and their successors, through whose discourse the Church was enlarged, etc., *as far as* nor was the Hebrew name translated, because this wood is scarcely found among other peoples.

VERS. 16.-- *Every one shall have ten cubits in length, in breadth a cubit and a half. [n: (BEDA, ibid.)]* The length of the boards is the height, which had ten cubits, etc., *as far as: and I will show him my salvation [n: (Psal. XC)]*.

VERS. 17.-- *In the sides. [n: (ID., ibid.)]* It is mortised in the sides of the boards, etc., *as far as* we receive in the bosom of love, and believe that we are received by them.

VERS. 18.-- *Of which twenty shall be on the south side. [n: (ID., ubi supra, cap. 6.)]* How great the length of the tabernacle was, etc., *as far as* lest anyone think that virtues [0273B] without work suffice him.

VERS. 19.-- *For which thou shalt cast forty sockets of silver,* etc. [n: (ID., ibid.)] The bases by which the boards were held up are the words and the books of the law and of the prophets, by which the apostles and evangelists proved that the things they willed to write and to preach were true and divine. Whence: *Then was fulfilled that which was spoken by the prophets.* And elsewhere: *Now all this was done, that the scriptures of the prophets might be fulfilled [n: (Matth. XXI)]*. [var: Vulg. these words stand at Mt 26:56; the address Migne prints points to Mt 21:4, which reads *ut adimpleretur quod dictum est per prophetam dicentem*] And Peter says: *We have the more firm prophetical word [n: (II Petr. I)]*.

[n: (BED., GREG.)] By the silver bases of the boards the order of the prophets is signified: who, when they first spoke of the Lord's incarnation, rose as it were as bases from the foundation, and sustained the weights of the fabric set above them, [0273C] and they sustain the boards, that is, the apostolic preaching spread abroad in the world, as firm and molten bases: because the life of the apostles is instructed by their preaching, and is made solid by their authority. Whence two bases joined together are set under each board: because while the prophets agree in their words concerning the incarnation of Christ, they build up the preachers that follow, so that, in the point where they do not differ among themselves, they may fix those others the more strongly. They are commanded to be cast of silver. For the brightness of silver is preserved by use, and without use turns to blackness; but the sayings of the prophets, before the coming of Christ, were not in the use of spiritual understanding, since they could not be beheld through their obscurity, and therefore they remained as it were black: but after Christ, coming, wiped them before our eyes with the hand of his incarnation, [0273D] whatever of light lay hidden in them shone forth, and he gave the understanding of the preceding fathers into use, because he expounded the words by the things.

[n: (BEDA.)] Rightly are two bases set under each board, that the consent of the prophetic attestation in all the things which the apostles said may be shown. Or they are put beneath, two by two, at the two corners, that, the corners being well propped, the whole board may be able to stand upright: because the beginning of all apostolic and evangelical discourse is prefigured in the prophetic books, and the end of the whole life of the apostles and of their successors, from the beginning of faith unto the end of the present life, is inserted in the mystical boards of the old testament.

VERS. 20.-- *On the second side also.* The southern side, which looks toward the south wind, signifies the ancient people of God, [0274A] which, long ago receiving the light of the knowledge of the law, glowed with the love of God. The second side, which looks toward the North, signifies the Gentiles, who until the time of the incarnation were torpid in the darkness and cold of unbelief; of whose calling it is said: *I will say to the North: Give up; and to the South: Keep not back [n: (Isa. XLIII)]*. As if to say: I will say to the people of the Gentiles, long benumbed without faith: Let there come of thy sons to the faith of my confession and of my love. Yea and to the people of Israel, which already enjoys the light of my knowledge: Keep not back the Gentiles from being received into the lot of election.

*Twenty boards.* [n: (BEDA.)] Now the board on either side was of one number, and of one measure, and of one making: because one and the same faith, hope and charity is preached to either people through the apostles, and to the promised land [0274B] of the heavenly kingdom either people is called. Of both they receive a general precept: *Going into the whole world, preach the Gospel to every creature [n: (Marc. XVI)]*, that is, to uncircumcision and to circumcision. And without any difference it is subjoined: *He that believeth and is baptized shall be saved.*

VERS. 21.-- *Two sockets.* A base is a foundation, from the Greek βασεία, that is, I found. By the bases the prophets are signified, upon whose authority the apostles and all doctors lean. And they are called two because of the concord of the prophets, who all thought one thing.



## CHAPTER XXVI. (cont.)

VERS. 22.-- *Toward the west,* etc. [n: (BEDA, ibid.)] The western quarter, etc., *as far as* they are to remain in the Church unto the end of the world.

[0274C] *But toward the western quarter,* etc. [n: (ID., ibid.)] Rightly is the tabernacle completed in the western quarter, etc., *as far as* but also for the evils from which he delivered them.

[n: (ID.)] Rightly does the western quarter, which is said to look toward the sea, consist of six boards: because in the number six the consummation of a good work is marked; because in it the Lord consummated the adorning of the world; in it he created man in the beginning; in it he restored the human race by his passion. Or because there are six ages of the world, in which we must be perfected by good works, that we may come to the eternal glory and to the glory of the resurrection.

VERS. 23.-- *And again two others.* [n: (ID.)] That again two other boards, besides the first six, are commanded to be raised up at the corner behind the back of the tabernacle, [0274D] which receive the wall coming from the eastern quarter and are joined to the wall of the western quarter, pertains to the reward of the life to come, which after the labours and the times of one life is secure, or made secure, being divided into a sabbath-rest, that is, the rest of souls after they are released from bodies, and the glory of the resurrection common to either people, to be ended by no end.

VERS. 24.-- *And they shall be joined to one another. [n: (ID., ibid.)]* [var: Vulg. Ex 26:24 *Eruntque conjunctae a deorsum usque sursum*, "and they shall be joined together from beneath unto the top"; Migne's *sibi* makes the joining mutual] Because the whole life of the elect tends by one faith and charity toward the things of heaven, and comes to the one end of the divine vision, and in one voice of right doctrine all the discourse of the holy preachers agrees. For the framing of the boards would fall apart from one another, if what one prophet or Apostle had said, another denied

[0275A] *And to the two boards also,* etc. [n: (BEDA, ibid.)] The corner boards are joined in all things to the boards of the walls, etc., *as far as* the prophets foretold with a consonant voice that these things should be.

[n: (ID., ibid.)] Here the measure of the bases is not stated, etc., *as far as* but that it might be kept unmoved in secure quiet.

*Thou shalt make also bars of wood,* etc. [n: (ID., ibid., cap. 7.)] The five bars which hold together the boards of the tabernacle, etc., *as far as* nor did he lay upon them other things than the commandments of the law.

VERS. 29.-- *The boards themselves also thou shalt overlay with gold [n: (ID., ibid.)]* The gilded boards of the tabernacle shine forth, etc., *as far as* but they gather also the light of the supernal recompense.

*Which thou shalt cover with plates. [n: (ID., ibid.)]* The bars are covered with plates of gold, etc., *as far as* when the spiritual [0275B] understanding is shown full of mysteries.

VERS. 30.-- *Thou shalt set up the tabernacle. [n: (ID., ibid.)]* The pattern of the tabernacle, etc., *as far as* they compose both their work and their mind.

VERS. 31.-- *Thou shalt make also a veil. [n: (ID., ibid., cap. 8)]*. This agrees, even according to the letter, with the beauty of the heavenly vision. For if thou consider the comeliness and the flames of the stars, the manifold appearance of the clouds and the rainbow itself, drawing out a thousand and diverse colours when the sun is turned away, thou shalt mark in heaven many more and fairer paintings than in the veil of the tabernacle.

*Thou shalt make also a veil,* etc. [n: (ID., ibid.)] This veil, *as far as* that he may appear before the face of God for us.

*Thou shalt make also a veil of hyacinth and purple and scarlet twice dyed. [n: (ID., ibid.)]* Thus far concerning the southern wall, [0275C] etc., *as far as* colours when the sun is turned away.

VERS. 32.-- *Which thou shalt hang before four,* etc. [n: (ID., ibid.)] Now the four pillars, etc., *as far as* this shall not be there where there is nothing at all of evil.

*Which indeed shall be gilded. [n: (ID., ibid.)]* The pillars before which the veil is hung are gilded, because the powers of the angels, placed within the veil of heaven, are clothed with the grace of the highest brightness. They have heads of gold, because they have the mind by which they are ruled made bright by the presence of divine knowledge and vision.

[n: (ID., ibid.)] The pillars have bases of silver, etc., *as far as* the brightness of words is shown by the silver.

[n: (GREG., Moral. lib. XXVIII, cap. 6)]. He commands the bases of the four pillars to be of silver, etc., *as far as* they carry the sayings of the evangelists by mouth and by work.

[0275D]VERS. 33.-- *And the veil shall be inserted,* etc. [n: (BED., ubi supra. )] Because it was in the eternal counsel of the Godhead, etc., *as far as: in the day of judgment.*

*Within which thou shalt put the ark of the testimony,* etc. Within this veil the ark of the testament was placed, because Christ, conscious of the Father's secrets, after his passion and resurrection, ascending the heaven of heavens, sits at the right hand of the Father.

*They shall be divided.* Because the Church, which consists of angels and of men, partly sojourns in the lowest places, partly reigns in the highest, has her citizens still separated one from another, the veil of heaven parting them.

VERS. 35.-- *And the table without the veil,* etc. [n: (ID. ibid.)] The table and the candlestick of the tabernacle signify the temporal [0276A] benefits of God, etc., *as far as* bearing witness to him.

VERS. 36.-- *Thou shalt make also a hanging,* etc. [n: (ID., ibid., cap. 9)]. The narrative concerning the veil being set forth, etc., *as far as* because it rejoiced in the continence of flesh and of mind.

[n: (ID.)] According to the letter the tabernacle was a house consecrated to God, etc., *as far as* of breadth fifty.

VERS. 37.-- *And five pillars. [n: (ID., ibid.)]* The pillars by which the hanging is suspended, etc., *as far as* the evangelical Scripture not yet laid open.

*Of setim wood. [n: (ID., ibid.)]* The pillars of wood, etc., *as far as: but Jesus Christ.*

*The heads whereof shall be of gold,* etc., [n: (ID., ibid.)] The golden heads signify Christ, etc., *as far as* it is built up of the souls of the elect [0276B] that lack corruption.



## CHAPTER XXVII.

VERS 1.-- *Thou shalt make also an altar of wood. [n: (BED., de Tabernac., lib. II, cap. 11, tom. II.)]* Namely because clean and uncorrupted ought those hearts to be in which the Holy Spirit dwells.

*And three cubits. [n: (ID., ibid.)]* Such, well nigh, is the stature of a man, etc., *as far as* it does not pertain to the altar.

VERS. 2.-- *And the horns,* etc. [n: (ID., Ibid.)] The altar of holocaust has four corners, etc., *as far as* it abides inviolable, nay rather the stronger.

VERS. 3.-- *And thou shalt make for the uses thereof cauldrons. [n: (ID., ibid.)]* The divers vessels of the altar, etc., *as far as* charity, never to be quenched, shall burn.

[0276C] *And tongs. [n: (ID., Ibid.)]* The tongs are the preachers, etc., *as far as* they bid also to shine.

*And fleshhooks, and firepans,* etc. [n: (ID., ibid.)] The fleshhooks, which in Greek are called κρεάγραι, etc., *as far as* they should go beyond the measure of our capacity.

*Receptacles of fire.* For this were they appointed, that by them the holy fire might be carried from the altar of holocaust to the altar of incense, evening and morning, for the setting on of the incense; because the teachers carry fire as it were from the altar of holocausts to the altar of incense, when by teaching they advance their hearers from virtue to virtue, and, as their merits grow, to higher things and to the more inward secrets of the divine vision. And they who imitate the hearts of their neighbours fervent with piety become receptacles of fires, because the flame of the heavenly sacrifice, [0276D] which they behold in their brethren's minds, they are busy to kindle in their own.

VERS. 4.-- *And a gridiron after the manner,* etc. [n: (BED., ubi supra, cap. 18.)] The whole altar was commanded to be made hollow, etc., *as far as* we think they learned it from the teachers of the Jews.

VERS. 5.-- *Beneath the hearth,* etc. [n: (ID., ibid.)] The altar has a hearth, etc., *as far as* they may be brought to the end of perfection.

[n: (ID.)] If it moves anyone according to the letter, etc., *as far as* it may be found the cleaner.

VERS. 6.-- *Thou shalt make also bars.* The teachers, namely, who may call the Jewry and the Gentile world into one grace of faith with harmonious mouth and work. Or, on either side of the altar [0277A] there shall be bars for carrying it, when the preachers shall teach the minds of the faithful to hold the constancy of the virtues in prosperity and in adversity.

VERS. 8.-- *As to thee [sic: Sicuttibi] in the mount. [n: (BEDA, ibid.)]* All the things which for us mystically, etc., *as far as* we are bidden always to do.

VERS. 9.-- *Thou shalt make also a court. [n: (ID., ubi supra, cap. 13.)]* As the Holy of holies, etc., *as far as* but the memory of it is rooted out from the inmost heart.

*A hundred cubits. [n: (ID., ibid.)]* It is agreed that the number of a hundred, etc., *as far as* and they may take up the continence of things temporal.

VERS. 10.-- *And twenty pillars. [n: (ID., ibid.)]* The pillars on which the hangings are hung, etc., *as far as* because they sound forth nothing other than the word of God.

[0277B] *With bases,* etc. [n: (ID., ibid.)] By the bases of the pillars, etc., *as far as* they shall never lack the fruit of a reward.

*Of silver.* [n: (ID.)] The heads not silvered over, but wholly of silver: because the teachers wholly subject themselves to the divine Scripture, and by meditating and preaching the divine words make over to them soul and body. For as the members cannot live without the head, so they, accounting the divine oracles their own life, never cease to bear these by obeying, and to exalt themselves by humbling themselves.

VERS. 11.-- *In like manner also on the side. [n: (ID., ibid.)]* The same things are repeated concerning the side of the North, etc., *as far as* they are commanded to be made on one side of the tabernacle.

VERS. 12.-- *But in the breadth,* etc. [n: (ID., ibid.)] The breadth of the court which looks toward the setting of the sun, *as far [0277C] as* ten pillars lift up and bear it.

VERS. 13.-- *In that breadth also of the court. [n: (ID., ibid.)]* The breadth of the court looking toward the east, etc., *as far as: and the life of the world to come.*

*Fifty cubits shall be.* [n: (ID.)] By the fifty cubits of the eastern side are signified in general the beginnings of them that believe, because they are kept in the remission of sins and in the hope of future blessedness; and the particular distinction of these is subjoined, which, being well considered, is referred to the same end. For there follows.

VERS. 14.-- *In which there shall be hangings of fifteen cubits allotted to one side, and three pillars,* etc. [n: (ID., ibid.)] For fifteen, etc., *as far as* and to keep the body without complaint unto the day of the Lord.

VERS. 16.-- *And it shall have pillars,* etc. [n: (ID., ibid.)] [0277D] In the entrance of the court there are four pillars, etc., *as far as* they spend all the senses of the body in the obedience of charity.

*Of hyacinth,* etc. [n: (ID., ibid.)] Rightly in this entrance of the court of the hangings, etc., *as far as* they adorn the hanging woven with a fair variety of colours.

VERS. 17.-- *All the pillars,* etc. [n: (ID.)] What he had said he inculcates the more diligently, that he may earnestly admonish the reader that none can be reckoned among the teachers of truth, that is, the pillars of the heavenly court, who shall not have learned to have, after the likeness of brass, an unconquered patience amid adversities; unless he shall have adorned himself by fencing himself about on every side, as with plates of silver, with the observance and the announcing of heavenly words; unless he shall have put himself under the divine [0278A] precepts by every subjection, as under a head of silver, and shall have made himself steadfast in the fear of God as upon a base of brass.

VERS. 18.-- *In length,* etc. [n: (ID.)] A hundred cubits of length, etc., *as far as* and it shall be made of twisted fine linen, etc.

VERS. 19.-- *All the vessels,* etc. [n: (ID., ibid.)] These vessels are men in the Church, etc., *as far as the courts of the Lord.*

VERS. 20.-- *Command the children of Israel. [n: (ID., lib. III de Tabern., cap. 1, tom. II.)]* All the adornment of the tabernacle being disposed, etc., *as far as* it may show the light of good action.

*Of the trees of the olives. [n: (ID., ibid.)]* It is to be noted that the children of Israel are bidden to offer oil, etc., *as far as* for [0278B] the burning of the incense of prayers.

VERS. 21.-- *And they shall set. [n: (ID., ibid.)]* For it belongs not to all to preach the mysteries of the faith to the people, etc., *as far as* to such as James says: *Be ye not many masters, my brethren,* etc. [n: (Jac. III)].

*It shall be perpetual,* etc. [n: (ID., ibid.)] This clause, like those which to the tabernacle, etc., *as far as* since God is all in all.



## CHAPTER XXVIII.

VERS. 1.-- *Take also unto thee,* etc. [n: (BEDA, lib. III de Tabernac., cap. 1, tom. II.)] How the tabernacle, etc., *as far as* and all the masters of the Church.

[n: (ID.)] *Take also,* etc. The making of the tabernacle being described, [0278C] etc., *as far as* they are consumed by the fire of the vengeance from above.

VERS. 2.-- *And thou shalt make a holy vesture. [n: (ID., ibid.)]* The holy vestments of Aaron, etc., *as far as* and a dispenser of the word of God.

VERS. 4.-- *And these. [n: (ID., ibid.)]* How each of these was made, etc., *as far as* they ought to be excellent and glorious to the sight of the inward judge.

[n: (GREG. Past., lib. II, cap. 3, tom. III.)] The priest is bidden on either shoulder, etc., *as far as* as in the diverse fashion of the superhumeral the twisted fine linen shines white.

VERS. 6.-- *And they shall make the superhumeral. [n: (BED., lib. III de Tabernac., cap. 4.)]* Because on the shoulders we bear burdens, etc., *as far as* when through abstinence the flesh [0278D] is wearied.

*And scarlet twice dyed, and twisted fine linen, of embroidered work. [n: (ID., ibid.)]* The scarlet shines with the appearance of fire, etc., *as far as* showing the scarlet in his own habit.

VERS. 7.-- *It shall have two edges joined at the top on both sides, that they may be closed into one. [n: (ID., ibid.)]* Of this it is said in what follows, etc., *as far as* than that we seek the rewards of the invisible life.

VERS. 8.-- *The very texture also and all the variety of the work shall be of gold and hyacinth and purple, and scarlet twice dyed, and twisted fine linen. And thou shalt take. [n: (ID., ibid.)]* Not gold in one part, etc., *as far as* the priest ought to be bright.

VERS. 9.-- *Two stones,* etc. That the priest may imitate the faith [0279A] and life of the patriarchs, and be mindful of the twelve tribes in his sacrifices and prayers, and that the people, beholding, may not stray from the merits of the patriarchs.

VERS. 11.-- *Set in gold,* etc. [n: (BED., ibid.)] Fitly are the stones bidden to be set in gold and to be encompassed about, etc., *as far as* they may be able to slip away from the keeping of the mind.

VERS. 12.-- *And Aaron shall bear their names before the Lord upon both shoulders for a memorial.* Because the priest, diligently weighing at every hour the life of the saints that went before, ought in adversity and in prosperity to be fortified with the ornament of the virtues, so that, walking by the arms of justice on the right hand and on the left, he may be bent aside on no side of the lowest delight.

[n: (BED. ibid.)] For three causes the names of Aaron, etc., [0279B] *as far as* or may set forth the examples of the fathers which they are to follow.

VERS. 13, 14.-- *Thou shalt make also hooks of gold, and two little chains of purest gold, linked one to another, which thou shalt put into the hooks. [n: (ID., ibid.)]* These hooks were in the topmost corners, etc., *as far as* they are to be knit together by charity and by good intention.

[n: (GREG., Past., part. II, cap. 2, tom. III.)] It is commanded that upon Aaron's breast the rational of judgment be fastened with binding fillets, etc., *as far as* subjects are not ruled without great fear.

VERS. 15.-- *Thou shalt make also the rational of judgment with work,* etc. [n: (BED., ubi supra, cap. 5.)] As in the superhumeral the perfection of works, etc., *as far as* they may study earnestly to think what befits them.

[0279C]VERS. 16.-- *Double. [n: (ID., ibid.)]* The rational was made, etc., *as far as* that he may lay hold of the prize of the supernal vocation of God.

VERS. 17.-- *And thou shalt set in it. [n: (ID., ibid.)]* This setting of divers gems in the rational, etc., *as far as* that they too may deserve to be made members of the highest priest.

[n: (ID.)] We can in the varied comeliness of the stones, etc., *as far as* it profits him nothing.

VERS. 19.-- *The onyx.* [n: (ID.)] Rightly moreover the two onyx stones, etc., *as far as* he himself may advance to better things.

VERS. 22.-- *Thou shalt make in the rational,* etc. [n: (ID.)] The order of the work, etc., *as far as* and they would be moved apart one from another.

[n: (ID., ibid.)] The superhumeral the consummation of good [0279D] works, etc., *as far as* if he does not, hastening, hold it in with the hooks of good action.

[n: (ID.)] That the superhumeral was drawn tight with a belt, etc., *as far as* through the working of the virtues.

VERS. 28.-- *A hyacinth fillet,* etc. [n: (ID., ibid.)] The hyacinth fillets by which both habits of the priest are coupled are the bindings of heavenly desire, which, knit into the golden rings, draw the superhumeral together with the rational, when, the light of the everlasting fatherland being known, we sigh after that glory, and, that we may deserve to enter in, are earnest to serve in the tabernacle of the present Church with concordant faith, and life, and work, and profession.

VERS. 29.-- *And Aaron shall bear the names of the children of Israel in the rational of judgment upon,* etc. The names of the children [0280A] of Israel Aaron bears upon his breast as a memorial before the Lord for ever, when the prelate never intermits the care of his subjects, but strengthens their life by consoling, rebuking, exhorting, and is busy to commend it to the Lord to be guarded with frequent prayers.

[n: (GREG., lib. XXVIII Moral., cap. 9.)] The priest, when he enters the tabernacle, is bidden to bear on his breast twelve stones graven with the names of the children of Israel: this is our Redeemer, who, offering himself a sacrifice for us, while he had strong preachers at the beginning, bore twelve stones under the head, in the first part of his body.

It behoves the priest to bear the memory of the fathers on his breast, chiefly when he ministers at the altar, that in the ministry which is [0280B] the more sacred he may the more skilfully adorn himself with his whole study, with all the industry of mind and of body, looking upon the examples of the saints, that as their earnest imitator he may present himself fitted to the divine gaze.

VERS. 30.-- *And thou shalt put in the rational of judgment,* etc. [n: (BED., ubi supra. )] That the priest, namely, may remember, etc., *as far as* in that which he dispenses in God's stead.

*And thou shalt put in the rational,* etc. [n: (AUG., Quaest. 117 in Exod.)] In what thing or metal the doctrine and the truth are set upon the rational, etc., *as far as* set upon the λογίον.

VERS. 31.-- *Thou shalt make also a tunic,* etc. [n: (BED., ubi supra. )] The priest is clothed with a tunic reaching to the feet, wholly of hyacinth, etc., *as far as* it is rightly added:

VERS. 32.-- *In the midst whereof above there shall be a head-opening [0280C] and a woven border round about it: as it is wont to be made,* etc. The hyacinth tunic has a most firm head-opening, and a border woven out of itself, lest it be easily rent, when the beginning of a good work, propped by the strong root of the fear of the Lord, is proved to be fortified also against all the snares of the ancient enemy. Such a border clothes and adorns the priest's neck round about, when it affords to the ruler confidence in preaching heavenly things: this, that he has not only lived rightly in the course of his life, but has also begun his beginning from rectitude, like Samuel, Jeremias, John, who from their very infancy, filled with spiritual grace, were also set apart in the order of teachers. For because the voice is in the neck, by the neck is expressed the practice of right speaking, *as it is wont to be done in the outermost parts of garments:* as [0280D] by the garments the works, so by the outermost parts of the garments the consummation of the works is figured. Or, the outermost parts of the garments are our last anxieties, in which the faithful, when they are compelled to end their life, strive the more intently to purge themselves of every stain of evils, taking heed with fear and trembling lest, being brought before the strict judge, they be cast out into the outer darkness for the filthy habit of vices, but may appear clothed, as the elect of God, with bowels of mercy, benignity, modesty, patience and charity, which is the bond of perfection [n: (Coloss. III)]. The hyacinth tunic, therefore, has a border woven in the head-opening, as it is wont to be made in the outermost parts of garments: when an excellent teacher begins the work of the virtues from so great a perfection as another scarcely at any time arrives at by [0281A] long labour; and when with so great fear of service he walks at every hour with his God, as another, about to die and to enter upon the last judgment, scarcely suffices to have. But because the whole perfection of the priest consists in works and in the doctrine of truth, according to that: *Jesus began to do and to teach [n: (Act. I)]*, rightly is it subjoined: *And beneath at the feet of the same tunic [n: (Exod. XXVIII)]*.

*Thou shalt make also a tunic,* etc. [n: (BEDA, ut supra. )] The tunic is called of the superhumeral, etc., *as far as* that from the head even to the feet it ought to be covered by the grace of the virtues.

VERS. 33.-- *And beneath,* etc. [n: (GREG., lib. I Regist., c. 29.)] When the ruler prepares himself to speak, etc., *as far as* you may hold the unity of the faith with wary observance.

[n: (BEDA, ut supra. )] Beneath at the feet of the tunic there are made, [0281B] etc., *as far as* they disclose by the distinct sound of speech.

[n: (ID.)] We can understand in the twelvefold number of the gems of the rational, etc., *as far as* Of the same year David also says: *Thou shalt bless the crown of the year of thy goodness [n: (Psal. LXIV)]*.

[n: (ID.)] Or as was said above, etc., *as far as* in the splendour of divers virtues.

VERS. 35.-- *That it may be heard,* etc. [n: (GREG., ubi supra. )] The priest going in or coming back, etc., *as far as* the very works of the priest may cry out.

[n: (BED., ubi supra. )] But if we will attend to the words of Josephus, etc., *as far as* it might be preached to the parts of the foursquare world.

VERS. 36.-- *Thou shalt make also a plate,* etc. [n: (ID., ibid., c. 7.)] The golden plate on the forehead of the high priest, etc., *as far as* that [0281C] we may confess that she has risen again.

*Holy to the Lord,* etc. [n: (ID.)] That which is bidden to be graven on the plate, etc., *as far as* which he repaired by his passion.

[n: (ID.)] We bear on the forehead the Holy of the Lord graven in gold, etc., *as far as* who ought to show to all an example of virtue.

VERS. 37.-- *And thou shalt tie it.* The plate which contains the Holy of the Lord is tied with a hyacinth fillet to the tiara of the high priest, when by the hope of heavenly rewards, which the hyacinth signifies, we are strengthened in faith, and keep the more closely undefiled the mysteries of our redemption, or the image and likeness of our maker and redeemer, the more we have known that there is no other way of salvation. Of the tiara it shall be said in its own place. [0281D]

VERS. 38.-- *And Aaron shall bear.* The priest bears, namely, the iniquities in the gifts and offerings which the children of Israel have offered and sanctified to the Lord: when for the fruits of worthy penance, that is, for alms and the other works of justice which the penitent do, he absolves them from the guilt of their crimes. These things indeed the divine ordinance figured in the habit of the high priest as to be so performed, and taught manifestly in words. But, alas for the grief! certain men receive offerings, nay rather exact them, and do not carry away nor chastise the iniquities of the people, when for this alone ought they to take carnal things, because they minister spiritual ones. But because he can labour effectually for his subjects, and preach, and beseech the Lord, whose [0282A] mind is always furnished with the memory of the divine name, it is fitly subjoined: And the plate shall be always on his forehead, etc.

*And the plate shall be,* etc. [n: (BED., ubi supra, cap. 8.)] If the plate written with the name of God shall have been always on Aaron's forehead, etc., *as far as* they hasten to earn.

VERS. 39.-- *And thou shalt gird the tunic. [n: (ID., ibid.)]* Why the linen one is called drawn tight, etc., *as far as* when it is said: *And thou shalt make a tiara of fine linen,* etc.

[n: (BED., ibid.)] In what manner this was made, etc., *as far as* it may render it harsh and hard to the hearers.

*And the tiara,* etc. [n: (ID., ibid.)] The tiara, which is called cidaris and mitre, etc., *as far as* he might accomplish by the fear of God.

[n: (ID.)] Of the making of the belt, etc., *as far as* and the other garments, when it is said:

[0282B]VERS. 40.-- *But for the sons of Aaron thou shalt prepare linen tunics,* etc. [n: (Ibid.)] But it must be said of the belt of the high priest, etc., *as far as* and faith the girdle of his reins.

*Thou shalt prepare tunics,* etc. [n: (ID., ibid.)] The priests have linen tunics, etc., *as far as* and the smell may be worthily kept for God.



## CHAPTER XXVIII. (cont.)

VERS. 41.-- *And thou shalt vest with these,* etc. [n: (ID., ibid., cap. 9.)] With all these were Aaron and his sons to be vested, but distinctly, so that he indeed should use all of them; but the sons the last three, which are properly ascribed to them, when it is said: *But for the sons of Aaron thou shalt prepare linen tunics,* etc.

[n: (ID.)] The breeches, which are made for the covering of the shame of the body or of the flesh, etc., *as far as* with the breeches, [0282C] namely, the linen drawn tight, the girdle, and the tiara.

## CHAPTER XXIX.

VERS. 1.-- *But this also thou shalt do,* etc. [n: (BEDA, ubi supra. )] Thus far of what sort the habit of Aaron and of his sons ought to be, etc., *as far as* they show figuratively.

*Take a calf.* [n: (STRAB.)] Whosoever is high priest or priest ought to offer a bullock, that is, firmly to believe and to imitate the passion of Christ; and two rams, that he may believe that the same Christ consists of body and soul; and unleavened loaves, that is, cleanness of life; and sprinkled with oil, that among all the virtues he may not neglect the works of mercy, or, not of himself, but of the gift of grace, may believe that he has them.

VERS. 2.-- *And a wafer.* [n: (BEDA, ubi supra. )] A wafer [0282D] sprinkled with oil we offer to the Lord for our consecration, when all the things which we do grow fat through the inward grace of the Spirit in the devotion of the heart. Cakes anointed with oil, when we show without all doubt that the things we do are spiritual, for an example of living; by which oblations our consecration is perfected, while through good works and pure thoughts we acquire for ourselves, by God's gift, the merit of holiness.

*And when thou hast washed the father,* etc. [n: (ID.)] They are bidden first to be washed, then to be adorned with vestments, because whosoever undertakes the office of the priesthood ought to wash himself with the wave of tears, that he may approach worthy to the divine ministry.

[0283A] [n: (BEDA.)] There might be understood in this washing the baptism of the sacred font, if it followed that one chosen to the priesthood should then first be washed with the water of baptism unto the remission of sins, and if the Apostle did not contradict, saying: *Not a neophyte, lifted up into pride, he fall into the judgment of the devil [n: (I Tim. III)]*. [var: Vulg. 1Tim 3:6 *Non neophytum: ne in superbiam elatus, in judicium incidat diaboli*, "Not a neophyte: lest, being lifted up with pride, he fall into the judgment of the devil"; Migne prints no *ne*, so the purpose clause has lost its conjunction]

VERS. 5.-- *Thou shalt clothe Aaron,* etc. [n: (ID.)] It is to be noted that whereas in Exodus Aaron is asserted to be clothed with eight vestments, etc., *as far as* because he came in the name of the Father, saying: *I am in the Father and the Father is in me: and he that sees me, sees the Father also [n: (Joan. IV)]*. [var: Vulg. Jn 14:10-11 *ego in Patre, et Pater in me est* joined to Jn 14:9 *qui videt me, videt et Patrem*; Migne's own citation names Joan. IV]

VERS. 9.-- *After that,* etc. [n: (AUG., quaest. 25 in Exod.)] Another reading, etc., *as far as* to be sanctified by Moses.

VERS. 10.-- *Thou shalt bring near,* etc. [n: (AUG., quaest. 126.)] [0283B] Another reading, etc., *as far as* upon the calf to be immolated.

VERS. 18.-- *It is an oblation to the Lord.* [n: (AUG.)] An odour of sweetness to the Lord, etc., *as far as* draws in the odour with bodily nostrils.

VERS. 22.-- *And the right shoulder. [n: (GREG., Past., part. II, c. 3.)]* The priest by the divine law, etc., *as far as* he may invite to lofty things by the shoulder of work.

VERS. 30.-- *And he that shall enter in. [n: (AUG., quaest. 129.)]* How then does he say that it is proper to this one alone, etc., *as far as* and on both sides that would be kept which is written: *Mercy exalteth itself above judgment [n: (Jac. II)]*.



## CHAPTER XXX.

VERS. 1.-- *Thou shalt make also an altar,* etc. *(BEDA, ubi [0283C] snpra, cap. 11.)* The commands of the consecration being fulfilled, etc., *as far as* they draw near even in the body.

*An altar. [n: (ID., ibid.)]* Fitly also the altar of incense, by as much as it excelled in the brightness of its metal, by so much was it less in size: for the holier, the fewer.

*Of setim wood. [n: (ID., ibid.)]* Fitly of setim wood, like to the white thorn and incorruptible, etc., *as far as* it quickens with the varied grace of gifts.

VERS. 2.-- *Having a cubit. [n: (ID., ibid.)]* Of one cubit is the length of the altar and the breadth, etc., *as far as* he may spend the office of piety.

*Foursquare. [n: (ID., ibid.)]* Rightly the altar of incense, etc., *as far as* everlasting gladness shall be theirs.

*Horns. [n: (ID., ibid.)]* The horns signify the eminence of faith and [0283D] of virtue, etc., *as far as* having the appearance of piety, they deny the power thereof.

VERS. 3.-- *And thou shalt overlay it,* etc. [n: (ID., ibid.)] Fitly moreover it with brass, etc., *as far as* with how great sweetness of God they are refreshed in the hiding of his face.

*Both the gridiron,* etc. [n: (ID., ibid.)] Well the gridiron of the altar, etc., *as far as* it is rightly subjoined:

*Thou shalt make also to it a crown,* etc. [n: (ID., ibid.)] The altar of incense has a crown round about: in those who are lesser in merits, and dare not openly protest that the reward of the good fight and of servitude and of faith is laid up for them: yet they do all things with the intention of pleasing God well, but with the hope of receiving the reward.

VERS. 4.-- *And two rings,* etc. [n: (ID., ibid.)] There can [0284A] be taken in these rings the four books of the Gospel, etc., *as far as* the things that follow show: *And thou shalt set the altar,* etc.

VERS. 7.-- *And he shall burn incense,* etc. [n: (ID., ibid., cap. 12.)] Incense, or thymiama, etc., *as far as* let us render thanks.

[n: (ID.)] *And he shall burn incense,* etc. In the morning, the sun being risen, all things clearly, etc., *as far as* in a dark place.

*In the morning when he shall dress. [n: (ID., ibid.)]* In the morning Aaron the incense, etc., *as far as* and they delight to have leisure for prayers.

VERS. 9.-- *You shall not offer. [n: (ID., ibid.)]* In what follows, etc., *as far as* they water their couch every night.

[n: (ID.)] We can interpret these altars, etc., [0284B] *as far as* [sic: *uque ad*] it can be taken.

VERS. 10.-- *And he shall pray,* etc. [n: (ID., ibid.)] Because Christ our priest, etc., *as far as* he rightly subjoins:

*And he shall appease,* etc. [n: (ID.)] Aaron appeases upon the altar of incense, etc., *as far as* Isaac and Israel, etc.

VERS. 11.-- *And the Lord spoke,* etc. The altar of incense being described, there remains the description of the brazen laver, in which the priests about to enter the tabernacle should wash their hands and feet; but one command is set before it.

VERS. 12.-- *When thou shalt take. [n: (BEDA, ubi supra, cap. 13.)]* Of this precept David was forgetful, etc., *as far as* his own riches.

VERS. 13.-- *Half a sicle. [n: (ID., ibid.)]* That is, ten obols, etc., *as far as* it can by no [0284C] disturbance of the unbelieving be corrupted.

VERS. 14.-- *He that is counted in the number,* etc. [n: (ID., ibid.)] The number twenty, etc., *as far as* awaits in heaven.

VERS. 15.-- *The rich man shall not add,* etc. [n: (ID., ibid.)] Because whether a man be great in merits and perfect, or little, and set in the advancement of the virtues, upon all alike is laid the law of the decalogue, by which they should love God and their neighbour

VERS. 16.-- *And the money received,* etc. [n: (ID., ibid.)] This is done when whatsoever good we do, etc., *as far as* it shall be smitten with the stroke of strict vengeance.

VERS. 17.-- *And the Lord spoke,* etc. [n: (ID., ibid., cap. 14.)] There can in this laver or basin, etc., *as far as* which watched at the entrance of the tabernacle.

VERS. 18.-- *With its base. [n: (ID., ibid.)]* The base on [0284D] which the basin was set, etc., *as far as:* that you present your bodies, etc. [n: (Rom. XII)].

*To be washed,* etc. Christ also was washed with the water of this laver, before he entered in to offer at the altar, and burned the incense of his sacred body for our salvation on the altar of the cross: for out of love for us he shed tears, as at the raising of Lazarus.

VERS. 21.-- *Lest perhaps they die. [n: (BED., ibid.)]* For the death to be feared is the eternal one, etc., *as far as: if* he eats and drinks, etc. [n: (I Cor. XI.)]

VERS. 23.-- *Take to thee* [n: (STRAB.)] σμύρνα in Greek, in Latin is called myrrh, which is a tree in Arabia of five cubits, whose twigs the Arabians burn up, that they may sprout and bear fruit the better. From the reek of which [0285A] they would contract incurable diseases, did they not meet it with the smoke of storax. The gum of this tree is properly called stacte, and by misuse also myrrh.

*Of cane.* It is the shoot of the cinnamon, and it grows in India or Ethiopia, of two cubits only. And it is called cinnamon, because after the manner of a reed it has slender and folded canes, of an ashen or black colour; which, being broken, give back a visible breath, and the slender indeed is precious; but the thick, cheap and despised. The cane is an aromatic kind near Libanus. Of myrrh, of cinnamon, of cane, of cassia. These four kinds signify the four principal virtues, by which with the oil of unction, that is, with the grace of mercy, the Church of God is to be dedicated and sanctified.

VERS. 29.-- *And they shall be,* etc. *(AUG., quaest 135 in [0285B] Exod.)* When, namely, they shall have been anointed. What then shall now be the difference between those inner things which are covered by the veil?

*He that shall touch.* In like manner it was said above of the altar of sacrifices, which is so to be understood that it was lawful for the people to touch the tabernacle, when they offered victims, or any gifts whatsoever, and by touching they were sanctified, on account of that oil with which all things were anointed: not as the priests, who also, that they might discharge an eternal priesthood, were anointed from it.

VERS. 34.-- *Onycha.* Onycha is said to be a very small shellfish of sweet smell, of the size of a human nail: whence also it is called onycha in Greek, because ὄνυξ, onyx, in Greek is called a nail.

*Galbanum.* Galbanum is said to be the juice of a fennel-plant, and the best grows in Syria: it is gristly, [0285C] and less woody, and by its smell serpents are put to flight.

*And frankincense.* Thus in Greek, libanum in Latin it is called, from Libanus a mountain of Arabia: for there is another Libanus, of Phoenicia, in which are the cedars.

*Frankincense* the best, namely that which is called masculine, because after the manner of a human testicle it is round, whose tree grows in Arabia, in a withe tangled and twisted according to the quality of the air.

*Mystically* moreover these four kinds signify the kinds of the holy virtues.

VERS. 35.-- *And thou shalt make incense,* etc. [n: (GREG., lib. I Moral., cap. 39, tom. I.)] We make incense compounded of spices, etc., *as far as* it does not scatter from itself an odour more subtle.

[n: (ISID.)] Incense, which is compounded of four most odorous kinds ground to the greatest fineness, that is, of stacte, onycha, galbanum, and frankincense, signifies the prayers of the faithful; although these four may see the four elements to signify: so that frankincense, which is bright, may be compared to air, stacte to the waters, galbanum and onyx to earth and to fire, so that by these there may be an incense pleasing to God of all things which are in heaven and beneath heaven, and in the earth, and in the waters

*With work,* etc. [n: (AUG., quaest. 136.)] We ought not to think that an ointment is made, but incense or frankincense, which is set upon that altar of incense where it was not lawful to sacrifice, and it was within the holy of holies.

VERS. 37.-- *Such,* etc. [n: (RAB.)] We, namely, who ought not to use prayers and divine praises in the flatteries [0286A] of men. For God alone is so to be served, whence above it is forbidden that the flesh of man be anointed from it.



## CHAPTER XXXI.

VERS. 2.-- *Beseleel,* etc., who is interpreted *in the shadow of God.* As if he should say, *The Lord is my light and my salvation, whom [n: (Psal. LXII)]*. [var: Vulg. Ps 26:1 *Dominus illuminatio mea et salus mea: quem timebo?*, the very verse Migne's own next citation names as Psal. XXVI; his *Psal. LXII* points elsewhere] From him David is said to have drawn his origin, and the name of embroiderer, because Beseleel built the tabernacle with embroidered work.

VERS. 3.-- *And I have filled [n: (AUG., quaest. 128.)]* Whether those works are to be attributed to the gift of the Holy Spirit, which seem to pertain to handicraft? or whether this too was said significatively, so that they pertain to the divine Spirit of wisdom and of understanding and of knowledge, which are signified by these [0286B] things? Then also, whereas here he is said to be filled with the Spirit, that man with the divine Spirit of wisdom and of knowledge, the Holy Spirit is not yet read of.

VERS. VI.-- *Oliab,* who is interpreted *my protection,* as though he should say by his name, *The Lord is the protector of my life; of whom shall I be afraid [n: (Psal. XXVI)]*? And well do they build the tabernacle who so constantly trust in the Lord.

[n: (STRAB.)] It is to be noted that in the construction of the tabernacle the mystery of the Trinity is shown, as in the baptism and in the transfiguration. For in the baptism the Father sounded forth in the voice: and the Son was baptized; in the appearance of a dove the Spirit appeared. Ooliab also, who is interpreted *my protection, father,* the person of the Father: Beseleel also, who signifies *in the shadow of God,* the person [0286C] of the Holy Spirit: of whom it is said. *And the power of the Most High shall overshadow thee.* For by the power of the Most High the Holy Spirit is understood. Whence the Lord says: *I know that power has gone out from me [n: (Luc. VIII)]*. But the bidding, that is, the word by which the tabernacle was bidden to be made, is the Son, through whom the Father works all things in the power of the Holy Spirit.

VERS. 13.-- *See,* etc. In vain do the Jews glory in the legal observance. For it is openly said that it is a sign of that which is to come, namely of rest, and of blessedness; or of the rest of the saints in the present, because they rest from the tumult of worldly works and of vices.

VERS. 18.-- *Two tables [n: (AUG., quaest. 140.)]* Whereas God spoke many things, two tables only of stone are given to Moses, which are called the tables of the testimony; [0286D] because all the other things which God commanded are understood to hang upon those ten precepts which were written on the two tables, if they be diligently sought out and well understood; as those ten themselves upon two, namely the love of God and of the neighbour: on which, namely, *the whole law hangs and the prophets [n: (Matth. XXII)]*. [var: Vulg. Mt 22:40 *In his duobus mandatis universa lex pendet, et prophetae*, "On these two commandments dependeth the whole law and the prophets"; Migne has *tota* for *universa*]

## CHAPTER XXXII.

VERS. 1.-- *Make us,* etc. Gods in the plural, although one calf only was made, after the likeness, namely, of the ox Apis, whom they worshipped in Egypt; but he who cultivates idolatry subjects himself to all vices, and serves as many gods and demons as vices.

VERS. 2.-- *And Aaron said to,* etc. *(AUG., quaest. 141 [0287A] in Exod.)* Aaron is understood to command hard things, that in this way he might call them back from that purpose. Yet that very hard thing was done, that there might be gold for the making of the idol: which I thought was to be noted on account of those who are grieved if they are bidden to have any such thing divinely done for eternal life, or to bear it with an even mind.

VERS. 6.-- *And the people sat down to eat,* etc. [var: Vulg. Ex 32:6 *sedit populus manducare*; Migne prints *comedere*] Eating and drinking drove them to play, and play to idolatry: because if the fault of vanity is not warily restrained, the unwary mind is devoured by iniquity. Whence: *He that despises little things, falls little by little.* [var: Vulg. Sir 19:1 *qui spernit modica paulatim decidet*, "he that despiseth small things shall fall by little and little", a future; Migne has the present *decidit*, and reverses the order of the first two words] For if we neglect to care for small things, being insensibly seduced, we boldly perpetrate even greater ones.

VERS. 8.-- *These are thy gods. [n: (AUG., quaest. 142.)]* [0287B] The Lord, showing to Moses what the people had done concerning the calf and concerning the idol which they had made out of their own gold, says that they had said: These are thy gods, O Israel, who brought thee out of the land of Egypt. Which it is not read that they said; but God showed that this was their mind, for they bore in their heart the sense of these words, which could not lie hid from God.

VERS. 10.-- *Let me alone,* etc. [n: (GREG., lib. IX Moral., cap. 11.)] What is it to say to a servant, etc., *as far as* he could not temper the anger of God for himself.

[n: (ID., lib. XX Moral., cap. 6.)] It can build up the reader according to the history, etc., *as far as* nor mercy of discipline.

VERS. 13.-- *Remember Abraham, Isaac,* etc. He brings in the fathers, who are loved, on behalf of the sons, who offend, [0287C] and do not deserve to be heard; because the good things of the holy fathers often come to the aid of the sons.

VERS. 14.-- *And the Lord was appeased that he would not do the evil.* [n: (AUG.)] By malice he wished punishment to be understood here, as is that: The malice of their going out was esteemed. According to this both good and evil are said to be from God, not according to the malice by which men are evil. For God is not evil: but he brings evils upon the evil, because he is just.

VERS. 19.-- *And being very angry. [n: (ID., quaest. 144.)]* Moses, being angry, seems to have broken the tables of the testimony written with the finger of God. Yet by a great mystery the repetition of the New Testament was figured, because the old was to be abolished, and the New to be established. It is indeed to be noted with what great supplication he laboured to God for the people, [0287D] who was so severe in taking vengeance upon them.

VERS. 20.-- *And catching the calf which they had made. [n: (ISID. in Exod., tom. V.)]* The body of the devil is signified in the calf, etc., *as far as* he was swallowed up.

*And he gave it to the children of Israel to drink.* The Hebrews hand down that, when the children of Israel drank the water infected with the dust of the calf, those who had committed idolatry bore the dust of the gold upon their beards, by which sign also the guilty or those free of the crime appeared, and the guilty were slain.

VERS. 24.-- *And they gave me,* etc. [n: (AUG., quaest. 145.)] He spoke compendiously, etc., *as far as* he does not refute.

VERS. 25.-- *For Aaron had stripped him unto the ignominy [0288A] of filth and among his enemies,* etc. [var: Vulg. Ex 32:25 *spoliaverat enim eum Aaron propter ignominiam sordis, et inter hostes nudum constituerat*, "for Aaron had stripped him by reason of the ignominy of filth, and had set him naked among his enemies"; Migne has *in ignominiam*] It is to be noted that what the people did is attributed to Aaron himself, because he consented to the doing of what they had ill demanded. For it is rather said, Aaron stripped them, because he yielded to them, than that they stripped themselves, who demanded so great an evil. With the ornaments out of which the idol had been fashioned, either because he had shown their evil will which lay hid before, or because he had lost the help and protection of God.

VERS. 27.-- *Let every man put his sword,* etc. [n: (GREG., Past. p. III, c. 26.)] The sword, etc., *as far as* Whence it is subjoined:

*Let every man kill his brother,* etc. He killed those whom he found to be punished by the sword of rebuke, and he does not spare those whom he loves by kinship of the flesh. If therefore he is called of God who is stirred [0288B] by the zeal of divine love to smite vices, assuredly he denies himself to be of God who, so far as suffices, refuses to rebuke the life of the carnal.

VERS. 28.-- *And they did,* etc. [n: (AUG. in Locutionibus, locut. 124.)] He put the plural for the singular, etc., *as far as* and these manners of speech are interposed by fixed rules.

VERS. 31.-- *Or forgive them,* etc. [n: (AUG., quaest. 147 in Exod.)] He says this indeed securely, etc., *as far as* that he might pour out those words to God on their behalf.

VERS. 35.-- *Which Aaron made. [n: (AUG. quaest. 148.)]* It is asked: since Aaron was said above to have stripped the people, etc., *as far as* that which the Apostle says, crying out: *For how unsearchable are his judgments:* etc. [n: (Rom. XI)]. [var: Vulg. Rom 11:33 *quam incomprehensibilia sunt judicia ejus*, "how incomprehensible are his judgments," and without *enim*; Migne has *inscrutabilia*] [var: Vulg. Ex 32:35 *quem fecerat Aaron*, a pluperfect; Migne has the perfect *fecit*]



## CHAPTER XXXIII.

[0288C]

VERS. 1.-- *And he spoke,* etc. [n: (AUG., quaest. 149 in Exod.)] God being angered, etc., *as far as* he terrified them with his voice.

*Go,* etc. [n: (ID., quaest. 150.)] Straightway, as though to Moses himself, etc., *as far as* from the face of the Lord.

VERS. 2.-- *I will send a forerunner. [n: (AUG., quaest. 150.)]* God is with the just by visiting, by showing mercy, and by strengthening; but with the impious by avenging and by punishing. And the sense is this: With these I cannot be for thy sake, so as to avenge and to punish; nor for their sake, so as to visit and to strengthen: I will at least send my angel.

VERS. 4.-- *The people hearing.* [n: (RAB.)] He says that the people heard a most evil [0288D] word, that is, a most cruel and most sorrowful one. For what greater benefit can be bestowed upon anyone than to have God's grace present, and to acknowledge his Maker to be favourable to him; or what greater peril is there than to endure the wrath of his Author? He has nothing of good who shall lack the grace and the presence of his Creator.

VERS. 7.-- *Taking the tabernacle,* etc. This is to be taken by way of prolepsis, if it is taken of that tabernacle which was shown to Moses in the mount, and made, the Lord enjoining it, by Beseleel and Ooliab. Or else it was another tabernacle, to which they were already assembling for judgment.

[0289A]VERS. 8.-- *And when he went out,* etc. [n: (GREG., hom. 13 in Ezech.)] The Hebrew people, freed from Egyptian servitude, etc., *as far as* they endeavour to go forward.

*And they looked,* etc. [n: (STRAB.)] Moses holds the figure of the law. The Jewish people therefore looked upon the back of Moses, because it willed to understand nothing mystical, nothing spiritual in the law, but only the surface of the letter. Therefore his face was hidden, that is, the true and pure knowledge. When he had entered the tabernacle, the pillar of cloud came down and stood at the door. Another rendering says that when Moses wished to enter the tabernacle, the cloud set itself before him and hindered him. Here, however, it is said that when he had entered, the cloud spoke with him before the door. It is to be known, therefore, that he was entering [0289B] some part, namely the court, and then, before he entered into the tabernacle, the cloud set itself against him: in this place, therefore, it signifies those who are less perfect in knowledge and in work, and are not strong enough to penetrate the inmost things of the Scriptures. But elsewhere, when he entered into the midst of the cloud, he held the figure of the perfect, namely of those who search out the secret things of the mystical senses.

*And the Lord spoke.* Scripture speaks according to the opinion of the people, who thought that Moses spoke mouth to mouth with God, whereas he was speaking and appearing to him through a subject creature, that is, through an angel and a cloud. For his substance neither of men nor of angels (as it is) could anyone see, whence: *But to the King of ages, immortal, invisible,* etc. And elsewhere: *He dwelleth in light inaccessible,* etc. *(I Tim. [0289C] I, VI)*.

*[n: (GREG., in I Reg., cap. 10.)] The face of God* is the knowledge of him. Now God is known through a glass, and is known through himself. Through a glass here, through himself in heaven. But the glass is that light which God inhabits; and he himself is that light which he himself is.

*If therefore I have found grace,* etc. [n: (AUG., quaest. 151.)] By these words Moses shows, etc., *as far as* that they may keep themselves clean and inviolate, and may go forward.

VERS. 17.-- *And thee thyself I have known,* etc. *[n: (AUG., quaest. 153.)] Because I know thee above all men,* etc. [var: Vulg. Ex 33:17 reads *ex nomine*, "by name"; Migne prints Augustine's *prae omnibus*, "above all," and the question that follows is put only because of it] Does God know some things more and some things less? etc., *as far as* but it is to be sought more diligently in the earlier [0289D] parts of Scripture whether it be truly so.

VERS. 19.-- *I will show. [n: (AUG., quaest. 154.)]* When Moses had said to the Lord, etc., *as far as* the Lord Christ is called upon among all nations.

*And I will call. [n: (AUG., quaest. 154.)] I will call,* however, he said, not "I shall be called," putting an active verb for a passive, by an unwonted manner of speech, in which perhaps he willed to signify that he himself does this, that is, that it comes about by his grace that the Lord is called upon among all nations.

*And I will have mercy,* etc. [n: (AUG., ubi supra. )] That which he added, etc., *as far as* thus God declared that he would show mercy.

[n: (AUG., lib. de Praedestinatione et Gratia, cap. 10.)] [0290A]That men are called by God with no merits going before, etc., *as far as* if he who called should forsake them.

*Thou shalt not be able,* etc. [n: (AUG., quaest. 154.)] After the commendation of his mercy, etc., *as far as* one must die to this life.

VERS. 20.-- *For not,* etc. [n: (GREG., lib. XVIII Moral., cap. 37, tom. I.)] John also says: etc., *as far as* because satiety is ever kindled out of desire.

VERS. 21.-- *Behold,* he says, *there is a place with me, and thou shalt stand upon,* etc. [n: (AUG., quaest. 154.)] Again, when Scripture interposes the article and says, etc., *as far as* the faithful people stood upon the rock.

*[n: (GREG., lib. XXV Moral., cap. 16.)] There is a place with me,* etc. The place is the Church. The rock is the Lord; Moses, [0290B] the Jewish people, which did not believe the Lord preaching upon earth. It therefore stood upon the rock, beholding the back parts of the Lord as he passed by, because, that is, after the passion and ascension of the Lord, being led within the Church, it deserved to receive Christ by faith, and of him whose presence it did not see, it came to know the hinder parts.

*[n: (GREG. ibid.)] There is a place with me,* etc. The Church, namely, etc., *as far as* Of which solidity it is said: *Upon this rock I will build the Church.*

VERS. 22.-- *And when my glory shall pass,* etc. [n: (EUCHERIUS, lib. de Form. spirit.)] But God is said also to pass over, when out of the hearts of certain men, in which he was before believed by faith, afterwards, faithlessness creeping in or some offence whatever, he withdraws from them and passes over to others: as from the Jews to the Gentiles, [0290C] from heretics to Catholics, and from any irreligious persons whatever to the religious.

*And I will protect* [n: (AUG., ubi supra. )] *and I will cover with my hand over thee,* etc. [var: Vulg. Ex 33:22 *protegam dextera mea, donec transeam*, "I will protect with my right hand, till I pass by"; Migne prints Augustine's wording *tegam manu mea super te*], *as far as* then he weaves in what the Lord says afterwards: *Hew thee two tables of stone,* etc. [var: Vulg. Ex 34:1 *Praecide … tibi duas tabulas lapideas*, "Cut thee two tables of stone"; Migne prints *Excide*, "hew out"]



## CHAPTER XXXIV.

VERS. 7.-- *Who renderest the iniquity of the fathers to the children and to the grandchildren unto [sic: iu] the third and fourth,* etc., [n: (GREG., lib. XV Moral., cap. 22.)] Since it is written, etc., *as far as* who saw that they were following after evil.

VERS. 14.-- *The Lord is jealous. [n: (AUG., quaest. 158 in Exod.)]* For the Lord God is jealous, etc., *as far as* and he destroys every one that goeth a-fornicating from him.

VERS. 20.-- *The firstling of the ass,* etc. [n: (ISID.)] By [0290D] the ass uncleanness is designated, by the sheep innocence. To exchange, therefore, the firstlings of the ass for a sheep is to turn the first beginnings of an unclean life to the simplicity of innocence, so that, after the sinner has done those things which the Lord rejects as unclean, he may now by his doing bring forth such things as he may lay clean upon the sacrifice of God.

VERS. 21.-- *On the seventh day. [n: (AUG., quaest. 160.)]* When he was giving precept concerning the Sabbath, etc., *as far as* that at every season there must be a resting on the sabbath.

VERS. 24.-- *No man shall lie in wait [n: (AUG., quaest. 161.)]* That which he said, etc., *as far as* where God was to have his tabernacle or temple.

VERS. 25.-- *Thou shalt not immolate,* etc. [n: (AUG., quaest. 162 et 163.)] What is it that he says: Thou shalt not slay upon [0291A] leaven the blood of my immolated things? [var: Vulg. Ex 34:25 *Non immolabis super fermento sanguinem hostiae meae*, "Thou shalt not offer the blood of my victim upon leaven"; Migne's *immolatorum meorum*, "my immolated things," is the very wording the question that follows is built upon] Or does he here call those things his immolated things which are slain at the pasch, and give precept that there be no leaven in the house at that time, when the days of unleavened bread are come?

VERS. 26.-- *Thou shalt not boil a kid in milk.* [n: (AUG., ubi supra. )] Thou shalt not boil a lamb in the milk of its mother, etc., *as far as* but it is a historical narrative.

[n: (ISID.)] Moses fasted forty days, and Elias, and the Lord himself. For it is enjoined upon us out of the law, out of the prophets, and out of the Gospel. Which testimony it has from these. Whence upon the mount the Saviour shone forth in the midst between the two persons, that we may bridle our greediness away from all the allurements of the world as by the fast of temperance, so long as the perfection of the Decalogue is preached through the four parts of that same world, that is, to the whole globe, [0291B] so that ten multiplied four times may signify the number of forty.

VERS. 28.-- *And he wrote upon the tables,* etc. [n: (AUG., quaest. 166.)] It was said of Moses that he himself wrote, etc., *as far as* but by the grace of God.

VERS. 29.-- *And when he came down. [n: (ISID. in Exod., tom. V.)]* Moses went up again into the mount, etc., *as far as* whose image was in the tables.

[n: (ISID. ibid.)] When Moses came down with the tables, etc., *as far as* whence: *Open thou mine eyes, and I will consider the wondrous things of thy law [n: (Psal. CXVIII)]*.

VERS. 30.-- *But Aaron seeing,* etc. [var: Vulg. Ex 34:30 *Videntes autem Aaron et filii Israel cornutam Moysi faciem*, "But Aaron and the children of Israel seeing the horned face of Moses"; Migne prints a singular participle, and the gloss beneath it has them see *Moysen*, Moses himself, not his horned face — which is the reading Origen's homily is built upon] [n: (ORIG., hom. 12 in Exod.)] It is written that Aaron and all the children of Israel saw Moses, etc., *as far as* for the things which he speaks have glory, but it is covered and hidden, [0291C] and all his glory is within.

[n: (ORIG. ibid.)] Consider this also, that in the law the countenance of Moses is glorified, although it be covered with a veil, etc., *as far as* that we, freed from the servitude of the world, may seek after the liberty of knowledge.

VERS. 34.-- *And then he spoke to the children of Israel [sic: Isael] all things which had been to him,* etc. [n: (AUG., quaest. 172 in Exod.)] After Moses came down from the mount, etc., *as far as* that they might receive this name.

## CHAPTER XXXV.

VERS. 5.-- *Set apart. [n: (ORIG., hom. 13 in Exod.)]* Another reading: Take from your own selves a redemption for the Lord, etc., *as far as* and thy silver, that is, thy word, tried.

[0291D] [n: (ID.)] You know, who are wont to be present at the divine mysteries, etc., *as far as* all these things which are in us do them.

*Every one.* [n: (ID.)] Another reading: Each one of you, he says, etc., *as far as* so, if the master at his coming find anything of thine in his tabernacle, he claims thee for himself, and calls thee his own.

*Brass.* [n: (ID.)] It seems to be put for strength, etc., *as far as* Let all things therefore be offered to God: sense, speech, and voice.

VERS. 6.-- *Hyacinth.* [n: (ID.)] Let us see the rest: hyacinth, etc., *as far as* and you shall offer firstfruits to the Lord.

*And scarlet twice dyed. [n: (ID. homil. 13 in Exod.)]* Let us see why the scarlet is doubled, etc., *as far as* that [0292A] with the light of knowledge he may mingle a little fire of severity.

[n: (ID.)] If any man worthily understand the going out of the Hebrews from Egypt, etc., *as far as* and it designates nobler garments: *Put you on the Lord Jesus Christ, and make not provision for the flesh in its delights [n: (Rom. XIII)]*. [var: Vulg. Rom 13:14 *induamini … in desideriis*, "put ye on … in its desires"; Migne prints *Induite vos* and *in deliciis*, "in its delights"]

[n: (GREG., ubi supra. )] In the construction of the tabernacle, etc., *as far as* they gave forth a sound as it were without light.

[n: (ORIG., ubi supra. )] After these things the men received from their wives, etc., *as far as* whence: *All men take not this word, but they to whom it is given [n: (Matth. XIX)]*.

VERS. 6.-- *And goats' hair. [n: (ORIG. hom. 19.)]* Goats' hair is offered, etc., *as far as* he who offers to the Lord the skins of rams.

VERS. 21.-- *Whatsoever for the service,* etc. (GREG., *ubi [0292B] supra.* ) In the adorning of the tabernacle the men together with the women offer gifts, etc., *as far as* they are brought forth according as he himself shall have given.

VERS. 27.-- *But the princes offered stones,* etc. [n: (ORIG., ubi supra. )] The princes offered their gifts, etc., *as far as* that they may deserve to be set over the peoples.

[n: (ORIG. hom. 9 in Exod.)] The princes offer also oil that shall serve for the lamps and for the chrism, etc., *as far as* when the Lord shall have set thee in a green place, upon the water of refreshment.

VERS. 30.-- *Beseleel. [n: (RAB. in Exod., tom. II.)]* He is called a shadow, etc., *as far as* they strengthen their brethren by exhorting them in justice and judgment.

## CHAPTER XXXVI.

[0292C]VERS. 1.-- *Beseleel therefore made,* etc. [n: (RAB., in Exod. )] It is to be noted that not only from the royal tribe, etc., *as far as* he willed them to belong to the unity of his own body.

VERS. 2.-- *And when he had called them,* etc. [n: (ORIG., ubi supra. )] And after the people had offered, Moses, he says, called, etc., *as far as* and oughtest thou to fit it in its own place and bring it forth?

*And they who willingly,* etc. *[n: (AUG., quaest. 170 in Exod.)] And all who of their own will would go to the works, that they might finish them,* etc. [var: Vulg. Ex 36:2 *et qui sponte sua obtulerant se ad faciendum opus*, "and who had of their own accord offered themselves to do the work"; Migne sets the Vulgate lemma and Augustine's *vellent ire ad opera* side by side] Moses made mention of certain men, etc., *as far as* but liberally and of his own accord devoted.

VERS. 4.-- *Whereupon the craftsmen,* etc. [n: (AUG., quaest. 171)]. Note that the wise workers of the works were holy also in their manners: yet they could, if they had willed, have carried off much; but modesty forbade, or religion made them afraid.

## CHAPTER XXXVII.

[0292D]

VERS. 1.-- *And Beseleel made the ark. [n: (ISID. in Exod., tom. V.)]* In the ark of the testament there was a golden urn, etc., *as far as* flourishing always with the greenness of immortal memory.

VERS. 2.-- *A golden crown,* etc. Because in that the Church is spread abroad through the four parts of the world, she is preached as girt about with the four books of the holy Gospel, which nevertheless is enclosed within the compass of one crown, that is, within the firmness of the same faith, that is, its unity.

VERS. 4.-- *The bars also. [n: (ISID., ibid.)]* Because strong and persevering teachers are to be sought out as timbers that cannot rot, who, ever cleaving to the instruction of the sacred volumes, [0293A] may proclaim the unity of the Church, and, as it were with rings let in, may carry the ark; and they are commanded to be overlaid with gold also, that while they sound in the ears of others with their word, they themselves may also shine with the splendour of their life.

VERS. 6.-- *He made also the propitiatory,* etc. [n: (ISID., ibid.)] The propitiatory above the ark of the testament, etc., *as far as* because Christ is the head of the Church.

VERS. 17.-- *And he made the candlestick,* etc. Which signifies the Holy Spirit, who with sevenfold grace enlightens the Church that stands in the unity of faith; or Christ bearing the seven churches, in which the splendour of the sevenfold Spirit shines forth.

VERS. 23.-- *With the snuffers.* Which in Isaias are called tongs: namely the two Testaments, by which sins are purged, and which are joined together within themselves by the union of the Holy [0293B] Spirit.

## CHAPTER XXXVIII.

VERS. 8.-- *He made also a brazen laver,* etc. [n: (GREG., hom. 17 in Evang.)] Moses sets a brazen laver, etc., *as far as* that we may strive to go forward by those things by which we know that they whom we venerate went forward.

VERS. 20.-- *The pins also.* [n: (STRAB.)] The pins, according to the letter, were fixed at the top of the pillars, to which the cords were bound: which cords raised the curtains from the ground, and, being made fast above by certain little loops but below bound to the aforesaid pins, stretched out the curtains, that they might not draw into a wrinkle.

But mystically the cords signify Holy Scripture, of which Solomon says: *A threefold cord is hardly broken* [0293C] [n: (Eccle. IV)]: because Holy Scripture, consisting of history, morality and allegory, can be broken asunder by no doctrines of heretics. The pins, made after the manner of a tongue, signify the tongues of the teachers who expound Scripture. They raise the curtains also from the ground and stretch them out: because they teach their subjects to follow and to imitate heavenly things, and to draw no wrinkle of evil work or of deceit [n: (Ephes. V)].



## CHAPTER XXXIX.

. . . . . . . . . . . . . . . . . . . . . . . . .



## CHAPTER XL.

VERS. 1.-- *And the Lord spoke to Moses, saying: In the first month,* etc. [n: (STRAB.)] The first month of the year is Nisan itself, which is also April, in which the Jews [0293D] celebrated the pasch, and the tabernacle was set up. Allegorically, however, the first month signifies the beginning of grace, when the tabernacle of God, that is, the Church, was set up, when Christ, namely, being crucified, brought forth out of his side the mysteries of blood and water, by which the Church is dedicated and consecrated. And it is to be noted that this was the first month of the second year: for the first year was before the law, the second year under the grace of the Gospel, which the Lord blessed. Whence: *Thou shalt bless the crown of the year of thy goodness [n: (Psal. LXIV)]*. For it was the year of goodness, in which the salvation of the human race appeared through the humanity of Christ: in this year therefore the tabernacle of God was set up, that is, the Church founded and exalted.

[0294A] [n: (RAB. in Exod., tom. II.)] The first year is the whole time of the law, etc., *as far as* that the teachers of the Gospel may use these things in the confirmation of the faith.

VERS. 9.-- *Thou shalt anoint the tabernacle,* etc. [n: (STRAB.)] The tabernacle with its vessels is consecrated with the oil of unction; because whatsoever holy Church does with her virtues she ought to dedicate and consecrate to the grace of the Holy Spirit. For the oil of unction is compared to spiritual grace, by which the works of all the virtues are consecrated to the Lord.

VERS. 12.-- *And thou shalt bring Aaron,* etc. [n: (STRAB.)] The doors of the tabernacle, that is, of holy Church, are the apostles, through whom one must enter the court of heaven: to these are Aaron and his sons to be brought, that is, the whole company of the saints and chiefly of those set over them, that they may [0294B] imitate them and follow their footsteps.

VERS. 16.-- *And the bars.* The bar not only binds the walls together, but also strengthens the pillars of the entrance, that they fall not down, nor bend away from their upright standing; because the Lord has both joined either people together in one faith of love, and strengthens their teachers, that they may persevere in faith and in action and in preaching. Nor is it wonderful if by the heads themselves, and by the bar which holds those heads together, Christ is understood; because he is the head of every elect person by ruling him, whence: *The head of the man is Christ [n: (II Cor. XI)]* [var: Vulg. 1Cor 11:3 *omnis viri caput, Christus est*, "the head of every man is Christ"; these are the words Migne prints, reordered, but his own citation names II Corinthians]: and because he not only raises up single men to seek after heavenly things, but also holds all together, and, that they may not be bent away from their upright standing nor be parted from one another by discord, confirms them with the bond of peace. To which is like that the Lord is called both lamb, and shepherd, and door, [0294C] since the shepherd feeds the lamb, and both enter by the door; whereas properly he is the Word in the beginning [n: (Joan. I, X)]: but of these none is proper to him, but figurative.
